The serialized native lane then ran the whole live set against a real node at the widened tip and returned 32 of 32 in 293.2 seconds, which is the stronger statement: every confidential shape this workspace has ever accepted reproduced its identity through the changed code. A SILENT SUBSTITUTION WAS CLOSED ON THE WAY: the private lane's role map swept every unnamed role into `Balancing`, and a sponsor change arriving there would have asked the materializer to SOLVE its blinder. A solved blinder absorbs the input sum, so the remainder would have stopped being the sponsor's and the receipts would have stopped balancing -- one substitution producing two wrong outputs, and a candidate that is wrong rather than refused. THE FEE'S PLACEMENT IS DECIDED ON THE CODE RATHER THAN ON THE PROSE, and the two disagreed. `check_sponsor_region_amounts` states that the private lane declares its fee and its sponsor change as destination positions, and `private_destination_intents` carries a sponsored arm that gives a fee destination the RESERVE asset. That arm cannot reach an accepted candidate as written, and two clauses two layers below it are why: a fixture's fee output projects with no reserve asset, so `asset_carried` returns the case's PROTOCOL asset and the preflight asset comparison refuses a reserve-asset fee destination with `ProtocolAssetMismatch`; and `Fee` answers `is_a_protocol_member` with true, so the same output would be summed into the protocol created subtotal and add reserve units to a protocol tally. The sponsored fee therefore rides in `NonProtocolFundingRegion`, whose members are emitted as explicit outputs, held outside both balance equations, and checked against carrying the protocol asset and against colliding with a destination's program -- every clause of which this shape satisfies. The successor case declares THREE outputs and not four. The unreachable sponsored fee arm is recorded as a finding rather than repaired here, its repair being a vocabulary question this row does not own. THE SHAPE IS ACCEPTED AND THE ROW IS MOVED. A real node accepted and mined the sponsored CONFIDENTIAL with-change successor, 13873 bytes at weight 15490 in 19.2 seconds: a blinded sponsor coin in at an explicit asset, two blinded receipt destinations, a COMMITTED sponsor change in the reserve asset, and an explicit reserve fee in the non-protocol funding region and outside both balance equations. The bytes the node reported back are the bytes it was handed, an owner's signature verified against a message recomputed independently of the candidate, and the sponsor's committed change was located in the MINED bytes rather than assumed from what was sent. The blinded sponsor coin is censused by NAMED CHECK and never by a scalar, all four holding, the load-bearing one being that the commitment the chain holds is the one this workspace derives from published constants and no chain at all. TWO WALLS STOOD BETWEEN THE REGISTRY AND THE CHAIN AND NEITHER WAS THE REGISTRY'S, which is the disclosure this row owes most. The first: this ceremony seeded the owner message with the genesis identity in the order a target PRINTS a block identity rather than the order it HASHES one, and the node returned its own invalid-signature verdict while every consensus balance check passed -- the exact `DeploymentSeedInPrintedOrder` negative control this workspace had already measured, reproduced by accident and identified by the control that existed for it. That a wrong-preimage message and a balance-valid transaction are distinguishable at a node is what made the diagnosis a reading rather than a guess. The second: a sponsored request bears a fee by construction, so the candidate EXECUTES the covenant's fee clause, and the demonstration deployment is welded to a fixture fee digest that no program hashes to -- so the clause demanded of the fee output a value nothing can present and the node refused at OP_EQUALVERIFY. That is the defect the sponsor arc met on the explicit lane, met here for the same reason and answered the same way, by linking the FEE-BEARING vocabulary, whose taptree is its own; the demonstration's committed identity did not move to buy it. WHAT MOVED, IN ONE COMMIT WITH THE OBSERVATION: the section 15.2 `private-sponsor-values` row, `may_enter_the_delta` -- which hard-refused that class as the exit-gate ruling written in code -- and `A_SPONSORED_PRIVATE_SUCCESSOR_IS_REGISTRABLE`, now true with its inventory rewritten to say what the stop was and what answered it. The test that held the closed pair together now holds the open one, and it holds the gate against the accepted identity so a gate opened with no run behind it fails. THREE FINDINGS ARE FILED RATHER THAN REPAIRED HERE, each outside this row's subject. First, the sponsored branch of `private_destination_intents`' fee arm -- which gives a fee DESTINATION the reserve asset when the form is sponsored -- cannot reach an accepted candidate as written, being refused two layers below by the preflight asset comparison and by the fee role's protocol membership; it has never run, and whether it should be widened or removed is a vocabulary question. Second, NOTHING CROSS-CHECKS THE NON-PROTOCOL REGION MEMBER against the shape's own fee position, asset, or empty program: this candidate's fee landed at the index the covenant introspects because the materializer appends region members after the destinations and the arithmetic happened to agree, not because any clause required it, so a shape whose fee position was not last would build a candidate the covenant refuses with nothing between the two saying why. Third, `link_and_register`'s Demonstration arm still hands out the fixture fee digest, so any FUTURE sponsored ceremony that keeps that vocabulary meets the same OP_EQUALVERIFY wall for the same reason; the arm is correct for sponsorless demonstration work and the hazard is that sponsorship implies a fee without saying so at the link site. The executor's Python digest mirror is a fourth, narrower one: it knows role codes 1 and 2 only and frames one asset for every output, so it could not resolve a case carrying the new role -- which costs nothing today because its catalogue holds only the three one-asset predecessor and sponsor-coin cases and a successor never reaches it, and which would bite the moment a sponsored case had to be funded rather than merely submitted. |
| `T5-053` | DONE | Explicit-fee wave: the LAST cell of the owner fee matrix, the SPONSORLESS EXPLICIT transfer that pays its own fee, driven to an observed acceptance of its own shape. THE GAP WAS NOT WHERE THE FEE AXIS WAS, IT WAS WHERE THE FEE COULD BE DECLARED. The axis, the fee-bearing vocabulary and ruling R-C's conservation leaf were all already landed; what no request could do was SELECT a fee-bearing shape from the explicit lane, because the explicit entry point passed a hardcoded empty role census where the private lane censuses its openings. So the leaf that carries the fee as a TERM had been emitted at link time by a vocabulary nothing could reach and had NEVER been executed at a node, and this wave is the first request that ran it. THE DESIGN QUESTION THE GUIDE ANSWERED, not the design memo: section 12.3's may-not-select list names the target fee role and section 12.5 says a sponsorless request selects no fee-sponsor role, so a request-level fee amount was refused as a vocabulary change the guide forbids. The private lane had already answered it the other way and this wave copied that answer -- the fee is a DESTINATION ENTRY, which section 12.3 does admit, and its ROLE is declared beside the entry at construction time exactly as an opening's role is. The new `ExplicitDestinationRole` is that declaration with the confidential half removed rather than a reuse of `ConfidentialOutputRole`, whose members are blinding distinctions an explicit destination does not draw. THE CONSERVATION TERM TURNED OUT TO NEED NO CODE, and the reason is structural rather than lucky: because the fee IS a destination entry it is already inside the created total the request states, so `consumed == created` closes unchanged and a fee appended by construction would have been the case needing a term added back. Ruling R-C's leaf is unaffected -- it reads the fee at the fee position in the COVENANT, which is a different equality from the builder's. ZERO CHURN AT FORTY-FOUR CALL SITES: `finalize_live_transfer` became a wrapper delegating to `finalize_live_transfer_declaring` with an empty declaration, so one pipeline serves both and a self-paying candidate is not a parallel construction path that could drift from the one every other explicit shape uses. An empty declaration means every entry is a receipt output, which is what the other thirteen shapes say, and their runs build the bytes they built before. THE FEE IS THE PROTOCOL ASSET, mirroring the confidential lane's own rule at the same site: the fee is funded out of the consumed receipts, Elements balances per asset, and a reserve-asset fee beside no reserve-asset input dies at the tally. The sponsored fee keeps the reserve for the mirror-image reason. THE FOURTEENTH SHAPE IS NOT A SECTION 15 ROW and says so in its own type. Section 15.1's explicit positive table is complete at sixteen and a self-paying transfer is none of the sixteen classes, so `row_name` became an OPTION and this shape answers None -- the same shape the confidential ceremony's row_name has, for the same reason. A test asserts EXACTLY ONE shape names no row, so a second one acquiring the absent case is loud rather than invisible. The collision census keeps counting ten identities across the thirteen row-naming shapes and additionally checks that the fourteenth's identity collides with none of them, which is the property the census exists to protect: one acceptance may never be cited for work it did not do. THE NODE TOOK IT ON THE FIRST RUN and the run is REPRODUCIBLE -- the standalone drive and the full serialized lane produced the same identity bit-for-bit. One receipt of 5000 consumed, one destination of 4750 created and a fee of 250 paid with no sponsor anywhere in it, 782 bytes submitted and the mined bytes read back EQUAL to them, the single input's signature verified against an independently recomputed message, target-reported weight 1304, 6.0 seconds of wall time. THE TRANSACTION-VERSION FILING IS ANSWERED BY RUNNING AND DISCHARGED BY KEEPING THE VERSION. This is the first sponsorless form to face relay on its own; its predecessors paid no fee and travelled as package children, which is why the ABI builds a sponsorless form at the topology-restricted version. The candidate was offered to `testmempoolaccept` at that version with NO package parent and the mempool ALLOWED it, a block then including it. So the premise was narrower than it looked and the version is right anyway: what the topology-restricted version restricts is unconfirmed ancestry and descendants, and a transaction with neither satisfies those restrictions trivially rather than by exemption. Travelling as a package child was one way to satisfy them and not the reason the version was chosen. The claim is held by a test that recomputes the version off the built transaction rather than by the comment that discharges it. ONE MEASUREMENT GAP CLOSED IN PASSING: the explicit ceremony recorded no weight at all though the adapter had been reporting one, so section 20.5's comparison had nothing to compare on this lane. The record now carries the TARGET's figure, read off `decoderawtransaction` rather than computed here. NO CENSUS ROW MOVED AND NONE WAS INVENTED: the shape census is over BLINDED shapes and has no slot for an explicit-lane fee shape, so the evidence surface is the run of record and this row, as the brief directed. Every recorded digest re-derives untouched and that is a RUN rather than a claim -- the fee-bearing vocabulary is a SEPARATE deployment, so the demonstration taptree and every identity recorded against it are unmoved, and a test asserts only this shape leaves the demonstration vocabulary. Verified entirely on the server per the no-host-compute ruling, every step timed. |
| `T5-054` | DONE | Crossing wave: both REPRESENTATION-CROSSING directions of the ruling, each driven to an observed acceptance of its own shape. The fee matrix is complete and this wave leaves it alone; what is open is that a live transfer's representation is ONE variable for the whole transaction, so an explicit-valued receipt may not be spent into a blinded destination nor a blinded receipt into an explicit one, though the target admits both and this workspace's own predecessor funding performs the first one every ceremony as a FUNDING step rather than as a covenant-governed transfer. DIRECTION ONE is entry-blinding: explicit receipt inputs into TWO OR MORE blinded receipt outputs, two being the floor because a single blinded output is the zero-blinder degeneracy the fixture registry refuses as `DegenerateBalancingScalar` and Elements' own wallet refuses for the same reason -- a convention about confidentiality that both sides hold rather than a consensus rule, and the register's escape analysis stays as it is. DIRECTION TWO is exit-unblinding: blinded receipt inputs into explicit destination outputs beside the BLINDED ABSORBER that a nonzero input blinder sum requires, the absorber's blinder solved to that sum by the machinery the merge wave already proved. The absorber is a DECLARED DESTINATION POSITION and not a fourth output family, which is the ruling this wave is executed under: an absorber outside the destination range would carry the protocol asset outside that range and falsify the closure argument the whole induction rests on, while a declared position leaves the closure untouched and is checked by the leaf that declared it. Guide section 6.5 says mixed representation is unsupported UNLESS SEPARATELY ADMITTED, so the ruling IS that admission and no guide amendment is sought; the compiler's `MixedComposition` deferral is NARROWED to per-reference composition within one side rather than deleted, its ground surviving the wave literally true because the crossing introduces a per-side variable and still no per-reference one, and both negative safety rows keep refusing for their own stated reasons. The CONFIDENTIAL-TO-FEE-ONLY corner stays SOURCE-DERIVED-IMPOSSIBLE and is not attempted. Each direction moves only on a real acceptance at the pinned node -- funded, submitted, offered to `testmempoolaccept` before the mine, mined bytes read back EQUAL, signatures verified against independently recomputed per-input messages -- and the absorber's facts are censused by NAMED BOOLEAN CHECK and never by a scalar. Any layer that cannot pass takes a TYPED STOP with its path filed rather than being narrated around. THE EXIT DIRECTION IS ACCEPTED: two blinded receipts consumed, two EXPLICIT destinations created and one blinded absorber beside them, 5412 bytes submitted at a target-reported weight of 6561, the mined bytes read back EQUAL to them and an owner signature verified against a message recomputed independently of the candidate, in 11.5 seconds. It is the first transaction this workspace has built whose consumed and created sides are read under DIFFERENT representation plans. THE CROSSING IS VISIBLE IN THE BYTES and not only in the construction: the output-witness proof census is zero, zero and 4174 bytes, because an explicit value admits no range proof and an explicit asset no surjection proof, so the two explicit destinations carry neither and the single blinded absorber carries the transaction's only proof -- a vector no homogeneous shape of that arity can produce, three proofs being the wholly private answer and none the wholly explicit one. THE ADMISSION IS THE GUIDE'S OWN CLAUSE RATHER THAN AN AMENDMENT: section 6.5 says mixed is unsupported UNLESS SEPARATELY ADMITTED, and its next sentence is why no run could ever have supplied the admission -- an ad hoc mixed transaction accepted by the target does not widen the ABI -- so only a ruling could, and the owner's is it. What the ruling admits is a PAIRING of the two plans section 6.1 states exhaustively, one per SIDE, so the plan census stays at two members and crossing introduces no third representation. `MixedComposition` is NARROWED and not deleted: what stays deferred is two references of ONE side disagreeing, and its ground `NoPerReferenceVariable` stays literally true and still re-derives from the analyzed program, because a side is not a reference. Both negative safety rows keep refusing for their own stated reasons. THE CONSTRUCTOR DERIVES ITS REPRESENTATION FROM THE CONSUMED SIDE rather than storing one beside the composition, and that is what keeps the destination table's key where it is: a coin is looked up by the plan that RECOGNIZES it, so a crossing deployment substitutes its crossing constructor at exactly that key and no table widens. A crossing deployment is its own deployment with its own taptree, exactly as the fee-bearing one is, so no identity recorded against the demonstration could move to buy it -- and a coin a crossing transfer creates stays spendable by an ordinary homogeneous one afterwards, which is what makes the crossing a step in a lifecycle rather than a cul-de-sac. THE ABSORBER IS A DECLARED DESTINATION POSITION, checked by a POSITIONAL value-form leaf requiring the explicit form at every destination but the declared one and the confidential form at that one. It is inside the destination range and adds no output family, so the section 10.4 closure argument is untouched -- and the memo's reason for that was REFUTED while its conclusion held: a protocol-asset output outside the destination range already exists, the self-paying fee, and the closure survives it; the real bar is that the exact output count and the three-family position census leave no room for a fourth kind of position at all. Its blinder is not chosen and not stated: with no output deriving one there is nothing to subtract, so the registry's solve returns the consumed blinder sum ITSELF, which is why the pair must not cancel and why this shape spends the merge's own non-canceling predecessor. The pattern census declared the private form while the coordinator emitted the crossing one, leaving the crossing bytes unaccounted; the exit crossing's obligation now rides its OWN pattern identity, because it establishes MORE than the private form does -- which position carries which form rather than one answer for the whole range -- and the three obligations are a PARTITION over compositions rather than over plans. THE REGISTRY ROLE IS THE FEE'S OPPOSITE CORNER, at transcript code 6 and nothing else: a fee is defined by having NO program and an explicit destination by having one, and every role that existed answered at least one predicate wrongly for it. The count is now a stated axis, the explicit destinations are the leftover, a test requires the three to partition the output set, and the tally predicate learned the second way a sum lands: a shape with no blinded input presents a sum already zero. THE FULLY-UNBLINDING CORNER IS FILED AND NOT ATTEMPTED, and it is the case the register exists to keep apart -- both sides refuse it and for DIFFERENT reasons, consensus because a nonzero sum has nowhere to land and the registry independently because a manifest with no solving role has no output to solve. Its proof census is 4174 and 4174 bytes and the COUNT is the claim: a single blinded output would have been forced to a ZERO blinder, an explicit input contributing one, and its commitment would have hidden nothing. WHAT THE ENTRY DIRECTION NEEDED WAS NOT CONSENSUS but three first-party readings that had each conflated a SIDE with the transaction, and all three were found by RUNNING rather than by reading. A consumed receipt could not be stated without naming a registered confidential fixture output, though an explicit coin has no opening to name; it now carries an ABSENT opening and is checked against what it IS -- the value really explicit, the amount the one declared, the asset explicit -- with a COMMITTED value and no opening refused rather than read as explicit. The randomness rule keyed on the REQUEST'S plan rather than on the side being blinded, and so refused randomness to the one transfer that most needs it; randomness is what blinding consumes and blinding happens on the side a transfer CREATES, and while both sides were one plan the rule could read either and be right. The opening-binding census counted verified references against EVERY input rather than against the inputs that have them, demanding a reference for a coin with nothing to reference. THE ABSORBER FACTS AND THE ENTRY FACTS ARE BOTH CENSUSED IN BOOLEANS: the exit pair does not cancel, the entry coin carried no blinder, each proof census has exactly the proving entries its shape allows, both read-backs equal their submissions and both signatures verify. BOTH RUNS ARE REPRODUCIBLE: each crossing produced the same identity bit-for-bit in a standalone drive and in the full serialized lane, and a measurement gap was closed on the way -- the confidential ceremony recorded no weight at all though the adapter had been reporting one, so section 20.5's comparison had nothing to compare on this lane.
